## Build xl1 (2026-10-26; the live packet translator measured on the PSP model; not delivered; task #45)
- **What:** state hash 2's test build (`/home/claude/h2/d`) with the live translator of renderer_notes 11.12 beside the exact walk, timed as the region HOST_XL (window lines "translator", steps.csv `xl_us`, `prof A B xl`), switched by g_opt's OPT_XL (1024; `MGS2_OPT=fffffbff` off). `psp/xl1.sh` (steps d, emu, dump, model; work dir `$XL1` or /home/claude/xl1), `psp/xl_psp.c`; Makefile `XOBJS ?=` / `MAINX ?=` (empty: every other build is as before). main_psp.c: `NCFG` 11 -> 13 (a cfg cycle with ~512, the stage table off, indexed one past the table; never used on the PSP), the names "stage table off" and "translator off".
- **Exact in PPSSPP** on the deck route and the indoor route (on, switching, off); **model 110 ms per step on the deck tutorial and 62 ms indoors for the translator alone** (renderer_notes 11.12 "Step 1 on the PSP"). Not for the PSP as is.
- `xl_psp.c` after the runs: the report sums the translator's counters in 64 bits (a route passed 2^31 VIF1 words; `long` is 32-bit on the PSP) and says which counters are since the last start; compiled with -Wall, not yet in a build.

## State hash 2 (2026-10-26; in every build from v31 on; task #43)
- **Why:** a state hash (FNV-1a 64 of EE RAM + scratchpad, 32,784 KB, and of IOP RAM, 2,048 KB, a byte at a time through a 64-bit multiply) takes about 1.9 s on the PSP: battery.txt of v29 and v30 show 612 s between vb ~15,610 and ~16,320, which hold the 311 hashes of the dense range 15,700-16,010 and about 25 s of steps. A run takes about 450 hashes (311 dense, one every 500 vblanks, one every 100 in the timed range): about 14 of the 40 minutes of v29's and v30's runs, outside the step timer.
- **What:** `boot/boot.c` state_hash2 (at the end of the file, so the earlier functions keep their profile numbers): the same memory in the same order (with GUEST_SPR0: 16 KB of zeros for RAM 0..0x3fff, the rest of RAM, the scratchpad; then IOP RAM), a 32-bit word at a time in four lanes, h = rotl(h ^ w, 13) * P[j] (each step a bijection of the lane, so a lane that differs stays different unless a later word cancels the difference exactly), two fmix32 chains over the four lanes into 64 bits. The "hash:" line keeps its form and tools; the first hash of a run adds "state hash 2: ... in X ms, IOP RAM ... in Y ms; the FNV-1a 64 pair of before in Z ms" (that once costs one old hash). `MGS2_HASH_FNV=1` (dev) prints the old pair at every hash as "hash1: vb N ee X iop Y". On the PSP the loop is 22 instructions per 16 bytes (4 lw, 4 xor, 4 ror, 4 mult + mflo; psp-gcc puts three mflo right after their mult).
- **New references:** x86 build `/home/claude/h2/x86` (v30's x86 objects with the new boot_rt.o; ephemeral), `tools/dev/lean/runh.sh` on the four routes with `MGS2_HASH_FNV=1`. Every old pair, vfb and vuh equals the old references (scratch check.py): deck 252 / 252 and dense 559 / 559 against `_deck_v27`, crew deck 252 / 252 and 559 / 559 against `_w01a_v27`, free play 365 / 365 and 672 / 672 against `_free_v28`, indoors 378 / 378 and 685 / 685 against `_in_v30`: the same game states, so the new values are the references from v31 on: `prof/hashes_x86_{deck,w01a,free,in}_h2.txt` (md5 b7075686..., 67501..., 34fa8ad5..., 6742976d...) and `prof/hashref_x86_{deck,w01a,free,in}_h2.txt` (933e1b6e..., 976f9792..., 372827a6..., 1e447adf...). On x86: 7.6-9.9 ms + 0.6 ms against 46-49 ms for the old pair.
- **Emulator** (test build `/home/claude/h2/d` = v30's build d with the new boot_rt.o, PGO=use, v30's code order, relinked by rebuild_use.sh; `/home/claude/h2/emu.sh`; PPSSPP JIT, PPSSPP_VFPU_RN=1): deck ee / iop / vfb 63 / 63 against `hashes_x86_deck_h2`, 370 / 370 against its hashref, vuh 311, no split; indoors 127 / 127 against `hashes_x86_in_h2`, 434 / 434 against its hashref, vuh 311, no split. PPSSPP's own times: 149.3-150.7 + 9.1-9.7 ms against 1,323.7-1,332.1 ms for the old pair (8.3x).
- **Code order:** k_vblank_hook shrinks from 5,096 to 4,820 bytes (the hash loops leave it) and sh2_run, sh2_end and state_hash2 are new, so a relink with v30's order moves the 404 functions after k_vblank_hook in the order by -276 bytes and the unordered ones by +1,724 or +288. Each build redoes its profile and order, so v31's layout is its own as always.
- **Expected on the PSP [I]:** PPSSPP's ratio would give about 0.23 s per hash instead of 1.9 s, about 12 minutes less per run (about 27-28 minutes instead of 40). The first hash's line measures it.

## Build v30me5 (2026-10-26; the resting Media Engine, for the switch-offs; Ffm chose "Build it" on the card of 20:40 (tapped 20:43) after v30 run 5's log was read; build e 21:36, emulator-checked 21:42; described to Ffm and delivered 21:43 to Desktop\mg2 port\MGS2T_v30me5, all 15 md5s checked on the PC)
- **What it changes (only this; the game's code, route, mix, catcher 2 and battery log are v30's):** when the ring is empty for `ME_REST_AFTER` = 4 polls (a poll is ~75 us: run 5's idle rate 13,264 per second) the ME rests in HALT (`.word 0x70000000`) with IE + IM2 on, and the main CPU wakes it with one kernel write of 1 to `0xBC100044` (`hle_me_kick`) after a push that finds it asleep, once per rest (by `sleeps`). Handshake: the ME counts the rest, writes `asleep`, syncs and looks at `wr` once more (halts only if still empty, else "given up"); the main CPU writes `wr`, syncs and reads `asleep` (Dekker-like: at least one side sees the other's write). While the main CPU waits for the ME (full ring, barrier, end) it kicks again every 200 us that the ME rests with no progress (a lost wake; counted). The 2-s stall check stays: `me_dead` = split_stop + park, the game goes on exact with no mix (the main CPU does not take the mix over: A is state-only in every build). `MGS2_ME_REST=0` = v30's polling. The simulated mode (`MGS2_SPU_ME=2`) never rests.
- **Register writes (the card's three, nothing else new):** `0xBC300008` = 0x80000000 and `0xBC300000` = 0xFFFFFFFF once by the ME (`me_rest_init`, as mcidclan's meLibExceptionHandlerInit), the acknowledgement 0x80000000 to `0xBC300000` in the ME's handler (then reads until bit 31 is clear, at most 4,096), `0xBC100044` = 1 by the main CPU in kernel mode (also once in `hle_me_stop` after the exit request, so a resting ME leaves its loop). **New reads:** Sony's ME request words `0xBFC00600 / 608 / 700 / 710 / 714` (uOFW me_wrapper: request, its argument, state, the clock-change word, the AVC clock state) by the ME at every wake and at its start, and by the main CPU in kernel mode at the start ("me rest:" line) and in every battery line while the ME runs; scePowerGetBatteryRemainCapacity / FullCapacity and scePowerIsSuspendRequired in every battery line, scePowerGetForceSuspendCapacity / LowBatteryCapacity in the first (ME_DIAG5).
- **Sources:** `psp/me_rest.S` (new, 0x1a0 bytes, .text 2**2: me_rest_init, me_rest with the window me_rest_begin..me_rest_win_last = the jr's delay slot, me_rest_vec at its kseg0 alias in CP0 $25 (4-byte aligned, as mcidclan's aligned(4) handlers): any other exception jumps to the stub's vector saved in [17] (hle_me_fault); words [12..17] at 0xBFC00430-0x444), `psp/me_mix_me.c` (me_rest_once, rpc_copy), `psp/me_mix.h` (ME_REST fields: 224 bytes), `psp/me_mix.c` (CTL_BYTES in whole cache lines; g_rest, me_kick_check, wait_rd's rekick, the "me rest:" start line, a "mix vb N: ME rests ..." line every 100 vblanks in place of the idle-poll busy line, end totals), `psp/me/hle_me_mgs2.c` (k_kick, k_rpc), `psp/v26me_bat.c` (BAT_LINE 1400; the rest words and the capacity / suspend words), `psp/v30me5.sh` (from v30me.sh: -DME_REST on me_mix.c, hle_me, the ME unit (me_mix_me1.o + me_rest.o joined by psp-ld -r) and me_sim; -DME_DIAG5 -DME_REST on v26me_bat; title "MGS2 v30 ME rests (me5)", 23 characters as v30me's "MGS2 harness v30 ME mix"; compiles `psp/v30/main_psp.c`, v30's own main_psp.c (md5 886289d9...), because psp/main_psp.c changed after v30 (the first try stopped at the main_psp.o check)).
- **Build e** (/home/claude/v30me5/e, ephemeral; build d (21:20) had the window end at the jr; e adds its delay slot after the comparison with mcidclan's handler, which steps a Cause.BD EPC back by 4): pads 0x5e0 0x408 0x1dd8 0xa3c after 4 refits; elfcmp against v30's d: 3,016 text symbols +0, newlib / libgcc moved +0x120b8 (v30me +0x120f8: one cache line less), rodata / data / bss +0 modulo 8 KB as v30me; ovl_arena 0x08bb6000; catcher handler 0x08b5c8ac, screen 0x08b5c2cc, self-test load 0x08b5b12c. Objects equal to v30me's (/home/claude/v30/me) but hle_me.o, main_psp.o (the title string only), me_mix.o, me_mix_me*.o, me_rest.o, pad.o, v26me_bat.o. ME unit: nothing undefined, no rodata relocation, no GPREL. me_rest_init 0x08b54b08, me_rest_halt 0x08b54b6c, me_rest_vec 0x08b54b94, me_mix_work 0x08b5417c. md5: EBOOT 0b26a2af788c6ccb47ab613e901b3296 (d: fbdf9b3c...); the eight overlays = v30's.
- **Emulator** (PPSSPP cannot run the ME: the checks cover the game with the new objects and the new lines; `psp/v30me_emu.sh` with XCFG; /home/claude/v30/meemu/*_r5e*, ephemeral): r5e1 (`MGS2_SPU_ME=1`, `MGS2_ME_REST=1`, `MGS2_EXC_DRY=34650`; "mix: off (Media Engine not started: emulator ...)") and r5e2 (`MGS2_SPU_ME=2` simulated, `MGS2_ME_REST=1`, `MGS2_EXC_DRY=30000`), 330 / 340 s: both 127 / 127 against `prof/hashes_x86_in_v30.txt` and 434 / 434 against the hashref, the vuh lines (md5 d6490eec...) and all hash lines (cd948249...) identical to v30me's me1 / es2, no split; r5e2 75 / 75 sound checkpoints equal to the PC's, 75 "eq"; steps.csv 6,000 rows each, me_steps.csv 6,000 (r5e2); crash.txt 28 rows (the dry report; its code addresses +0x14b0, as the catcher's code moved); the battery lines carry the new words (the capacity and suspend reads return PPSSPP's error 0x8002013a, as its voltage read does; the simulated mode prints "rests 0 (...), may not rest, awake"); longest battery line 745 bytes (BAT_LINE 1400). Build d gave the same on both (r5a, r5s, 335 / 340 s). The ME's rest, wake and handler run first on the PSP.
- **Delivered** (21:43): EBOOT 0b26a2af788c6ccb47ab613e901b3296, mgs2.cfg 04850afd3fdac6f0fa9adcd6dbc07939 (v30's with `MGS2_ME_REST=1` and its comment, header rewritten; settings = the checked cfg) and README.txt efd56ef11ebf9b346c128fa40991d0e5 via /mnt/project-files/_stage (emptied after); the eight overlays, SLES_503.83 (e3f4c0df...), mgs2disc.pak (189ca53f...), hashref.txt (d666d226...) and pad.txt (7bc72ace...) copied on the PC from MGS2T_v30; memcard0 empty. All 15 md5s checked on the PC. Copies of the README and cfg: `prof/hw_v30me5/`. Asked run: the cheap battery, without USB, to the end (v30 run 1's conditions: stopped at 20.9 min with the polling ME; run 4's: finished with the ME off). Told Ffm 21:43, with the correction of the card (the stall check stops the mix and resets the ME; it does not hand the mix to the main CPU).
- **Reading the run:** "me rest:" (start; the request words as the kernel and the ME see them), "mix vb N: ME rests ..." every 100 vblanks (rests in the halt / before it / with no interrupt / given up, idle polls, kicks and their us, rekicks, flags, request words and their changes), "mix at the end: ME rests ..." totals; battery.txt adds the counts, the request words (ME and main) and capacity / suspend flag every 5 s. Healthy: rests mostly "in the halt", "no interrupt" 0, rekicks about 0, request words unchanged, mix 75 / 75. A wake that never comes shows as "mix: stopped (...)" after 2 s with "rests 1, in the halt 0".
- **PSP run 1** (Ffm 21:50: "it turned the console off"; log.txt, battery.txt, crash.txt in `prof/hw_v30me5/run1/`; no steps.csv / me_steps.csv: they start at vb 31,800): **the PSP switched itself off 90-92 s into the run, vb 3,170-3,200** (battery line t 90 s at vb 3,170 is the last; the log's commit at vb 3,200 (t ~92 s at this pace: v30 run 1 was at vb 3,178 at t 90 s) never landed), just after the r_tnk0 stage loads, in the heavy-logic stretch (vb 3,101-3,300: 67-80 ms of logic per step in every v30 run). The earliest stop of all: the ten earlier v30 runs (ME on and off) and every earlier ME build passed it. crash.txt: catcher on, self-test skipped (no answer in 15 s), no record. Until the stop, all as designed [C]: 3,343 rests, 3,341 ended in the halt by the main CPU's interrupt, 1 woken before the halt, 0 with no interrupt, 1 given up; kicks 3,342 (one per rest) and 0 while waiting; the ME's flags only ever 0x80000000; Sony's request words 00000187 000001ff 00000000 0000014d 00000002 at the start, at every wake and in every battery line (changed at 0 wakes); the game exact (hashes at vb 500-3,000: 6 / 6 equal to hashref) and the mix exact (checkpoints 0-5: 6 / 6 "eq", the same CRCs as v30 run 5); no "mix: stopped". Each kick costs 136-166 us on the main CPU (kuKernelCall), about one a step: IOP+sound +0.16 ms a step against v30 run 5's polling ME (vb 700-3,000: 1.40-1.58 against 1.24-1.41), the rest of the step equal. Battery: 100%, 4,206 -> 4,188 mV, 20 C, not charging, no external power, low 0, suspend required 0 throughout; capacity 1,688 of 1,790 mAh at the start, 1,672 at t 30 s, 1,653 at t 75 s; force-suspend capacity 72 mAh, low-battery capacity 216 mAh. Battery: the cheap one (Ffm 21:58; so the cheap battery's gauge reads 1,790 mAh full, the first capacity reading of any run). Told Ffm 21:56; card 21:56 "Run it again" (recommended: a stop near 1.5 min again ties it to that moment) / "ME off for now": Ffm 21:58 offered to run it on the OSTENT; asked for the cheap one again (one condition at a time), card closed "Run it again". Run 2: the same folder, cheap battery, no USB, to the end; if it stops near 1.5 min again, the OSTENT is the next test.
- **PSP run 2** (cheap battery, no USB; Ffm 22:02 "hash vb 5500", 22:21 "passed 20000 hash", 22:22 "still with the cheap batery"; files 2026-10-26 22:39 in `prof/hw_v30me5/run2/`: log.txt 2df28e9f.., battery.txt a306386e.., crash.txt 1b391995.., steps.csv eed3fab3.., me_steps.csv d8092222..; read with `tools/dev/me5_read.sh`): **RAN TO THE END, no switch-off**, 38.2 minutes (battery.txt "end" t 2,290 s at vb 37,800), on battery all the way (external power 0, charging 0, low 0, suspend required 0 on all 460 lines; 88% -> 73%, 4,152 -> 4,062 mV; the gauge 1,422 -> 1,188 of 1,790 mAh, -234 mAh, about 370 mA on average). Exact: ee / iop / vfb 127 / 127 against `prof/hashes_x86_in_v30.txt`, 434 / 434 against hashref, vuh 311 lines equal to v30 run 5's, no split; mix 75 / 75 checkpoints equal to the PC's, all "eq"; the ME stopped and acknowledged at the end. Catcher on, self-test skipped (O), no record. **The resting, whole run [C]:** 76,548 rests: 76,526 ended in the halt by the main CPU's interrupt, 7 woken before the halt, 14 given up (work came while it was going to rest), 0 with no interrupt; kicks 76,533 (137.1 us mean, max 161.4; 10.5 s in all) and 0 while waiting (no lost wake); the ME's flags only 0x80000000; Sony's request words 00000187 000001ff 00000000 0000014d 00000002 at every wake (changed at 0 wakes) and in every battery line. Kicks: 2.2 a vblank before the timed range, 1.07 a step in it. **Timing** (`tools/dev/steps_in.py` against v30 run 5's polling ME): 6,000 steps, mean 37.58 ms (logic 18.39, draw 13.58, IOP+sound 2.68, GS 2.93) against 37.41: +0.16 ms a step, all in IOP+sound (2.68 against 2.51: the kicks, 1.07 x 137 us), every part of the route +0.09..+0.23; max 96.34 (vb 37,634, as run 5's 96.00); the same 18 pack reads. Barrier waits over the run 4,355 ms (polling 4,653). **Tally with run 1:** the resting ME 1 stop in 2 runs (~0.66 h), the spinning ME 4 in 8 (~4.6 h), the ME off 1 in 27 (~13.3 h): resting cut the ME's own activity by about 95% [I] but not the stops, so the ME's load is now a weaker suspect than the ME being started at all [I]. Told Ffm 22:47; card "Study first" (recommended: no ME runs; compare our ME start with Sony's and known ME projects, then a concrete change and a short test plan) / "OSTENT run" (the same files on the OSTENT).

## Build v30me6 (2026-10-26 / 27; the Media Engine started as Sony's system starts it, for the switch-offs; task #52; after Ffm chose "Study first" on the card of 22:47 and the study `me_startup_study.md` was checked against the sources; builds a and b 23:47, emulator-checked 00:05; Ffm chose "All of Sony's" (build b) on the card of 2026-10-27 00:07 (tapped 00:33); b delivered 00:36 to Desktop\mg2 port\MGS2T_v30me6b, all 15 md5s checked on the PC; a not delivered)
- **What it changes (only this; the game's code, route, mix, resting ME, catcher 2 and battery log are v30me5's), both builds (`-DME_SONY`):** the stub is copied to `0xBFC00040` (`ME_STUB_AT`), where Sony's me_wrapper (uOFW me_wrapper.c:1095) and DaedalusX64 (main.c:133) put their ME code, and `0xBFC00000-0x3F` keep the kernel's shared start code (uOFW exceptionman excep.S `sub_0120`, copied by ExcepManInit when the kernel starts, exceptions.c:53-74): `ctc0 $v0,$6; mfc0 $v0,$22; bne $zero,$v0,+0x40; nop; cfc0 $v0,$9; jr $v0; nop` and 9 nops; the main CPU (id 0) goes to its NMI handler, the ME (id 1) branches to 0x40. Until v30me5 our stub overwrote that entry at the first start, so an NMI on the main CPU would have run the stub there (it writes 0xBC100040 / 50 / 04 and jumps into the ME loop) [I]; whether the main CPU takes NMIs in play is unknown (no uOFW module registers an NMI handler; dmacman clears bits 5 and 6 of 0xBC100000). Before anything is written, `k_entry` (kernel mode) reads the 16 words and compares the 7 that run with uOFW's, assembled by psp-as (`k_entry_want` 40c23000 4002b000 1402000d 0 40424800 00400008 0); any difference: the ME is not started (checked before the power lock: the run goes on as with the ME off, "mix: off (Media Engine not started: the start code at 0xBFC00000 is not the kernel's ...)"), and `k_start` checks again (-2: nothing written). The reset register `0xBC10004C` is changed with interrupts held off (`k_reset_set`: `mfic` / `mtic $zero` and 7 nops around the read-modify-write, as uOFW sysreg_driver.c:9-11 with usersystemlib intr.S) in k_start (hold, release) and k_park.
- **Build b only (`-DME_SONY_BOOT`), Sony's ME boot steps in the stub** (uOFW me_wrapper init.S:34-35, 60-74): `0xBC100050` = 7 (6 before; DaedalusX64 7), and the VME's reset and set-up: `0xBCC00010` = 1, a poll while its bit 0 is 1 (at most 1,048,576 polls, where Sony waits without a limit; the polls left go to progress word [7], 0xBFC0041C), then `0xBCC00070` = 1, `0xBCC00030` = 8, `0xBCC00040` = 2. Not taken from Sony's boot code: Status 0x20000000 (ours 0x30000000), discarding the ME's caches (ours writes them back on a warm start), the jump to the NMI handler when 0xBC100000 is not 0 (ours records the word).
- **Register writes:** a: none new (0xBC100040 / 50 / 04 by the ME and 0xBC10004C by the main CPU as before, v30me5's three for the rest; only the stub's place in the ME's boot memory moved). b: 0xBC100050 = 7 in place of 6 and the four VME writes, all by the ME: they need Ffm's OK (the card). **New reads:** the 16 words at 0xBFC00000 (kernel mode, at the start; "me start:" line), the ME's 0xBC100000 at its start (progress word [6], 0xBFC00418), and in every battery line while the ME runs (`k_sony`): the main CPU's 0xBC100000, 0xBC10004C and how many of the 7 words differ now.
- **Sources:** `psp/me/hle_me_mgs2.c` (ME_SONY: ME_STUB_AT, k_entry, k_reset_set, k_sony, hle_me_sony_words, hle_me_sony_line, the two checks in hle_me_start; ME_SONY_BOOT: the stub's boot steps), `psp/me/hle_me_mgs2.h` (the two prototypes), `psp/me_mix.c` (the "me start:" line after the "me:" line), `psp/v26me_bat.c` (the battery words), `psp/v30me6.sh` (from v30me5.sh: -DME_SONY on me_mix.c, hle_me and v26me_bat, and -DME_SONY_BOOT with SONYBOOT=1; titles "MGS2 v30 ME Sony (me6a)" / "(me6b)", 23 characters as v30me5's). Without the defines the objects are v30me5's.
- **Builds** (/home/claude/v30me6/a and b, ephemeral): pads a 0x70 0x1d8 0x1dc8 0x9ec, b 0x70 0x198 0x1d88 0x9ec, after 4 refits; elfcmp against v30's d: 3,016 text symbols +0, newlib / libgcc +0x120b0 (a) / +0x120b8 (b, as v30me5), ovl_arena 0x08bb6000. Objects equal to v30me5 e's but hle_me.o, me_mix.o, v26me_bat.o, main_psp.o (the title), pad.o; a and b differ in hle_me.o, main_psp.o and pad.o. Stub 0x160 bytes (a) / 0x1a8 (b) at 0x08bad060, position independent (relative branches only; absolute addresses only of the boot block, the progress words at 0xBFC00400 and EBase = hle_me_fault): at 0xBFC00040 it ends at 0x1a3 / 0x1eb, below the progress words. k_entry 0x08b4ebfc, k_reset_set 0x08b4ec68, k_sony 0x08b4ed60, k_start 0x08b4edc4, k_park 0x08b4ef70 (both). md5: EBOOT a 72d2eb2d8a7c5c6e7a1d2022b55dfa67, b 9ab4b3b289bd7b828f9b598205f4d382; the eight overlays = v30's (ovl_w01a 644c627a...).
- **Emulator** (PPSSPP cannot run the ME; `psp/v30me_emu.sh` with XCFG as v30me5's; /home/claude/v30/meemu/*_r6*, ephemeral): r6a1 / r6b1 (`MGS2_SPU_ME=1`, `MGS2_ME_REST=1`, `MGS2_EXC_DRY=34650`; "mix: off (Media Engine not started: emulator ...)"; "me start:" says 0xBFC00040 and -1 words, as hle_me_start stops at its emulator check first) and r6a2 / r6b2 (`MGS2_SPU_ME=2` simulated, `MGS2_EXC_DRY=30000`), 340 / 345 s each: all four 127 / 127 against `prof/hashes_x86_in_v30.txt` and 434 / 434 against the hashref, vuh 311 / 311, the vuh lines (md5 d6490eec...) and all hash lines (cd948249...) identical to v30me5's r5e1 / r5e2, no split; r6a2 and r6b2 75 / 75 sound checkpoints equal to the PC's, all "eq"; steps.csv 6,000 rows each, me_steps.csv 6,000 (simulated); crash.txt 28 rows (the dry report; its code addresses +0x568 (a) / +0x570 (b) against r5e1); the simulated battery lines carry the new words (all ffffffff: kuKernelCall fails in PPSSPP), longest line 833 bytes (BAT_LINE 1400). The new start runs first on the PSP.
- **Deliver files** (/home/claude/v30me6/deliver_a and deliver_b, ephemeral): mgs2.cfg = v30me5's settings with the header rewritten (a 6515d605..., b 32595f2b...), README.txt (a 1dc39bb0..., b b77c0169...). Delivery as v30me5's: EBOOT, mgs2.cfg and README via /mnt/project-files/_stage; the eight overlays, SLES_503.83, mgs2disc.pak, hashref.txt and pad.txt copied on the PC from MGS2T_v30; memcard0 empty; md5s checked on the PC.
- **Delivered (b, 00:36):** EBOOT 9ab4b3b289bd7b828f9b598205f4d382, mgs2.cfg 32595f2b754336280df835be7b888de6 and README.txt b77c01699d2c7d901fb88b572024820f via /mnt/project-files/_stage (emptied after); the eight overlays (= v30 me's: ovl_d00t 9b286ecb.., ovl_d01t a579aaa6.., ovl_init f4b9190d.., ovl_n_title 6ceee4b5.., ovl_r_title f6f459d8.., ovl_r_tnk0 102b6e32.., ovl_w00a 80aa55eb.., ovl_w01a 644c627a..), SLES_503.83 (e3f4c0df...), mgs2disc.pak (189ca53f...), hashref.txt (d666d226...) and pad.txt (7bc72ace...) copied on the PC from MGS2T_v30; memcard0 empty. All 15 md5s checked on the PC. Copies of the README and cfg: `prof/hw_v30me6/README_b.txt`, `mgs2_b.cfg`. Asked run: the indoor route, the cheap battery, without USB, to the end, up to 3 runs; a switch-off ends the test. Waiting for Ffm's run (log.txt, battery.txt, crash.txt, steps.csv, me_steps.csv).
- **Test plan (with the card):** the chosen build on the indoor route, the cheap battery, no USB, to the end, up to 3 runs. A switch-off ends the test and rules the change out as the whole cause. 3 clean runs are a hint, not proof: at the ME-on rate so far (5 stops in about 5.3 h, about 0.95 an hour) 3 clean 38-minute runs would come by luck about 1 time in 6 (e^-1.8). Read per run: the "me start:" line (the 16 words, 0 of 7 differing; the ME's 0xBC100000; b: polls left), the battery lines' main-CPU NMI word (any value other than 0 supports the NMI idea), the reset register, 0 words differing all run (nothing else rewrote the start code), and v30me5's rest counts and request words. Next if b stops: the study's item 2 (the SceMeRpc handler swapped as PS2RC does) or item 4 (the lock and the callback without the ME).

- **PSP runs 2 and 3** (run 2: cheap battery, no USB; Ffm's photo 2026-10-27 02:22 through the project chat, its files written over by run 3. Run 3: photo 02:31; log.txt, battery.txt, crash.txt 02:32 in `prof/hw_v30me6/run3/`; 39% at the start, the gauge 630 of 1,790 mAh (1,790 full: the cheap battery's gauge [I])): **both stopped with the crash catcher's record, no switch-off**: run 2 at sound mix vb 8,035 (about 16,960 wakes, from run 3's counts at the same vblank), run 3 at vb 11,931 (29,766 wakes at the last battery line, vb 11,910). Record of run 3 (run 2's equal but s6 0x01312d00, k1 0x8a90, Bad 4484bd98): COP0 and kernel EPC 0x08b4ed1c, Cause 0x10000424 (Bp, IP2 pending, BD 0), Status 0x60008603 (EXL, IE, kernel), Bad c41c5f02; zr 0, at 88068bdc, v0 0, v1 0, a0 0, a1 10000400, a2-t5 0, t6 60008601, t7 88067b90, s0 0, s1 00100000, s2 0b8dfa80, s3 08b4ed08, s4 0, s5 0b03d968, s6 002625a0, s7 09039a80, t8 0, t9 deadbeef, k0 08b4ed1c, k1 00008958, gp 08bb7f60, sp 88095600, fp 0b060000, ra 60008603, lo / hi deadbeef; EPC-12: 24030001 ac430044 0000000f 03e00008 00001025 3c02bfc0; ME mode 1, heartbeat 307,318, state 1, pushed 105,770, consumed 105,769, checkpoints 23, idle 213,376, last cmd 6 / 0x78, ME EPC 0, power switch 0, parks 0, flags 0xa0. Up to the stop run 3 was exact (ee / iop / vfb 23 / 23 against the hashref, mix 23 / 23 checkpoints eq) and rested as run 1 (by vb 11,800: 29,406 rests, 29,398 in the halt, 1 before it, 6 given up, 0 with no interrupt; kicks 29,399, 136.0 us mean; NMI word 0, start code words differing 0, reset register 0x40 in every battery line). **Cause [C]:** EPC 0x08b4ed1c is k_kick's `jr ra` (k_kick 0x08b4ed08: sync; lui v0,0xbc10; li v1,1; sw v1,0x44(v0); sync; jr ra; move v0,zero), run in kernel mode at its user address through ARK-4's kuKernelCall. The saved Cause (a1 = ctrl $3) 0x10000400 is an interrupt (code 0, IP2). uOFW interruptman start.S, intrExcepHandler: `cfc0 $ra,$2; cfc0 $a1,$3; cfc0 $k0,$0; cfc0 $k1,$23; andi $v0,$ra,0x18; sra $v1,$k0,31; or $v0,$v0,$v1; beqz $v0,loc_00000914` (kernel mode and EPC bit 31 clear) -> `j loc_00000914; break 0x3FF`; the break with EXL set keeps EPC and BD as the interrupt left them and makes the code Bp. Every register fits: v0 = v1 = 0, a1 Cause, ra Status, k0 EPC, k1 ctrl $23 (the last system call's code, kuKernelCall's: it differs per boot), sp the interrupt stack; t9 / lo / hi 0xDEADBEEF and at / t6 / t7 from the system-call path into kuKernelCall. ARK-4 (core/systemctrl/src/interruptman.c, patchInterruptMan) rewrites only the system-call handler's `break 0x3FE` (word 0x0003FF8D: a kernel-mode system call becomes a jump to the function, which is why k_start's two cache calls work in kernel mode), not this `break 0x3FF` (0x0003FFCD). **Why the same instruction twice [I]:** the CPU waits at the `sync` after the wake write until the write is done; an interrupt that comes during the wait is taken at the next instruction, the `jr ra`. Rate: 2 stops in about 203,000 wakes of the resting builds (v30me5 runs 1-2, v30me6b runs 1-3), about one in 100,000; a full run makes 76,454, so about every second run. Earlier builds had no wakes, only the 5-second reads (k_marks, k_rpc, k_sony: a few microseconds each in the same state). **The wake's cost [C]:** 136 us mean in every resting run because ARK-4's kuKernelCall calls sctrlKernelSetUserLevel twice, each a function search (sctrlHENFindFunction) and a full cache flush (sctrlFlushCache; core/systemctrl/src/sctrl.c and kubridge.c); a cheaper wake is a separate speed item. Told Ffm 02:46: the cause and the fix (build v30me6c).

## Build v30me6c (2026-10-27; v30me6b with its kernel-mode helpers at their kernel address, the fix for runs 2 and 3; task #55; built 02:47, described to Ffm 02:46, emulator-checked 02:55; delivered 02:57 to Desktop\mg2 port\MGS2T_v30me6c, all 15 md5s checked on the PC)
- **What it changes (only this):** `-DME_KADDR` on psp/me/hle_me_mgs2.c: kcall passes `fn | 0x80000000` (the kseg0 alias, the same memory) to ps2rc_kcall, as psp/v26me4_exc.c passes its handler (proven on the PSP: the catcher's handler drew runs 2 and 3's records from 0x88b5ce1c). All 12 kcall sites (k_entry, k_start, k_park, k_marks, k_kick, k_rpc, k_sony) form the alias before the call (checked in the disassembly: lui 0x8000 and or); the 3 other ps2rc_kcall sites already call kernel functions (the sleep hook's hookfn from ps2rc_exc.prx; ExceptionManagerForKernel's from sctrlHENFindFunction). The helpers hold only direct jal (which stay in the 0x8 segment) and jr ra; k_start's jal to the UtilsForUser stubs makes the same kernel-mode system calls as in b (ARK-4's jump; nothing on that path depends on the caller's address). No new register write, no new module. Script: `psp/v30me6.sh` with SONYBOOT=1 KADDR=1 (title "MGS2 v30 ME Sony (me6c)", 23 characters); without KADDR it gives b's objects.

## Build gl2d (2026-10-27; gl2 + system diagnostics in battery.txt, for the switch-offs; task #62; built 14:08; emulator-checked with gl2's delivery settings 14:08-14:18; delivered 14:45 after gl2 run 1's stop was read)
- **What it adds (only this; read-only, user mode, no register write, no new module):** `psp/v26me_bat.c -DSYS_DIAG` (script `psp/gl2d.sh`: gl2.sh with that flag; work dir /home/claude/gl2d, game folders MGS2GL2D_*; title "MGS2 harness gl2d system diagnostics"). (1) A power callback (thread "diag_power" 0x11, 0x2000; scePowerRegisterCallback(-1), slot 0 as fallback) writes "power event <flags>: <names>, level N" with the battery line the moment a notice comes. (2) A canary thread "diag_low" (0x6F, 0x1000) asks for a wake every 10 ms (sceKernelDelayThread(10000)); each 5-s line ends "low-priority thread: longest wait N ms, waits over 100 ms N, wakes N; thread switches N, out of idle N, idle N ms" (the last three: sceKernelReferSystemStatus deltas over the 5 s). (3) Every 60 s "threads <why>": sceKernelGetThreadmanIdList + sceKernelReferThreadStatus per id (name, current / initial priority, state, run time; "refused" and the error for an id user mode may not read). (4) The exits (psp/gl1_glue.c's sceKernelExitGame wrap) take the callback out (`me_bat_exit`). BAT_LINE 1600 with SYS_DIAG (else 1400 / 600 as before). scePowerGetBatteryElec not used (pspsdk: crashes in user mode).
- **Build** (/home/claude/gl2d/d, ephemeral): EBOOT 46b696fa1727d787501b83a33053afbe (3,910,232 bytes); v26me_bat.o 18,620 bytes (10,764 without SYS_DIAG, gl2's 10,600: `bat_line(why, reset)` with `me_bat_line` as its wrapper); the eight overlays relinked (d00t 93881dba.., d01t 6bebfff7.., init 26d16c6d.., n_title b04ec015.., r_title a30e420e.., r_tnk0 768ad4b2.., w00a acb99f89.., w01a 00fd60bc..).

(`prof/hw_gl2d/mgs2_gl2d.cfg` = gl2's cfg below a 3-line gl2d header), README.txt 137dc956.. - **Run 1 (Ffm's photo of the crash screen, 2026-10-27 15:21 in the project chat; files not yet sent): A CRASH CAUGHT BY THE CATCHER at vb 26,108**, the point where gl2 switched off (the second codec call's start). Screen: "sound mix vb 26108", COP0 EPC 0882696c Cause 1000001c (data bus error, user mode, interrupts on), the kernel's record the same; ra 0893bb38, v0 0, a2 = a3 = 00008000, a1 0b218520; the six words from EPC-12 lw t1..t3 / sw t4..t6 (memcpy's 32-byte loop); ME off. **Cause [C]:** EPC = memcpy+0x68 (`sw t4, 0(a0)`), called from sif_push (runtime/sif.h) right after `it->data = malloc(n)`: v0 = a0 = 0 and a3 = a0 + 0x8000 show malloc(32,768) returned 0, and the first store hit address 0. Caller chain from the stack: sifm_SetDma (the IOP module's sceSifSetDma). **Why the heap was short [C, x86]:** the IOP's codec load sends 1,110 KB in one IOP run (34 x 32 KB + 18 KB, alternating between two IOP buffers, with ~70 64-byte status words to EE 0x1c6d00, then one command): sif_push copied every piece into the heap and sif_ee_deliver handed them over only at the run's end, so 1,110 KB were held at once, at vb 15,681 and again at vb 26,108 (scratch x86 counter on route i to vb 27,000, hashes 270 / 270 = `hashes_x86_in_h2`; every other run held at most 514 KB). gl2's battery.txt: break 0b730000 at vb 26,000 against a heap end of about 0b818000 (headroom 6,608 KB at break 0b1a3000), so about 930 KB above the break; at vb 15,681 the break was 0b6f2000 (about 1,180 KB), which is why vb 15,681 passed. The emulator never saw it: PPSSPP's heap is 5,120 KB larger (11,728 KB at the start), and the "lowest heap headroom" lines sample once a vblank, missing peaks inside an IOP run. gl1's stop (after vb 15,759, in the dense-hash stretch) is NOT this one: gl1's battery lines at vb 15,733-15,759 show it ran past the vb 15,681 peak [C]. Fix: build gl2m below.

## Build gl2m (2026-10-27; gl2d + the IOP -> EE data fix for gl2d's crash; task #65; built 15:42)
- **The fix (runtime/sif.h, the only source change):** sif_push delivers a data item pushed during an IOP run (`sif_in_run`, set by sif_iop_run_body) straight into EE memory with ee_copy_to, after the data items queued ahead of it, when no command waits ahead; anything else queues as before (commands, data behind a command, pushes outside an IOP run such as padman's vblank DMA). The EE does not run inside sif_iop_run_body, so the EE sees the same writes in the same order as with the end-of-run sif_ee_deliver (the only possible difference would be an IOP-side read or direct write of the same EE bytes inside the same run: iop_host.h's cd_srv_ncmd / cd_srv_search writes and the mc write's ee_copy_from). The settle loop of sif_iop_run_body counts pushes with `sif_npush` (it counted sif_ft, which direct items no longer move). A failed queue malloc now prints "sif: no memory for N bytes of IOP->EE data" and stops (exit 7) instead of writing to address 0. Old file kept as /home/claude/sif_before_direct.h (ephemeral; md5 746c106b..).
- **x86 [C]:** `/home/claude/sifd/x86` (h2/x86's objects + the new boot_rt.o), `tools/dev/lean/runh.sh` on the four routes: deck 252 / 252 every-100 (ee iop vfb) and 559 / 559 dense against `prof/hashes_x86_deck_h2` / `hashref_x86_deck_h2`; crew deck 252 / 252 and 559 / 559 (`_w01a_h2`); free play 365 / 365 and 672 / 672 (`_free_h2`); indoors 378 / 378 and 685 / 685 (`_in_h2`). A counting build (scratch sifq2, route i, ran to vb 37,800, rc 0): 131,072+ items (92.7 MB) went straight in by vb 29,881; the queue's largest content in the whole run is 361,796 bytes in 5 items at vb 0 (boot, outside an IOP run, the same in the old code, which every PSP run passed); the old code held 1,110 KB at vb 15,681 and 26,108 and 514 KB at seven other points.
- **Build** (`psp/gl2m.sh d`: gl2d.sh with title "MGS2 harness gl2m memory fix", work dir /home/claude/gl2m, game folders MGS2GL2M*; ephemeral): EBOOT 64087201415846822e89b9495a724d3a; boot_rt.o and main_psp.o rebuilt against the same profiles; the eight overlays relinked (d00t e2f56bf9.., d01t 8708a53d.., init 3dd562e1.., n_title 750d435f.., r_title 4c090410.., r_tnk0 51e51716.., w00a 17f0c525.., w01a bbee3929..). gl2m.sh's emulator setup copies SLES_503.83 from /home/claude/mgs2 (the GAME folder MGS2V5 it used is gone; same md5 e3f4c0df..). New emulator run `hwmem` = hw + `MGS2_GL_TEXKB=7168`: a texture pool 5,120 KB larger, so the rest of PPSSPP's heap equals the PSP's (start headroom 11,728 against 6,608 KB); `B=<build dir> TAG=<suffix>` runs another build's EBOOT the same way (gl2d's for the reproduction).
- **Emulator [C]** (`psp/gl2m.sh emu hw hwmem`, and `B=/home/claude/gl2d/d TAG=_old psp/gl2m.sh emu hwmem` for the old build; started 15:47, 670-675 s each): **hw** (the delivery cfg) against gl2d's emulator run `/home/claude/gl2d/emu/log_hw.txt`: all 745 hash and vuh lines equal (ee / iop / vfb 127 / 127 against `prof/hashes_x86_in_h2.txt`, ee / vfb 434 / 434 against `hashref_x86_in_h2.txt`), the 52 picture lines (digests 31801-31899 and the totals' all digest) equal, the six shots equal pixel for pixel (`piccmp.py --same`), timing windows (`gl1_steps.py --skip 31801:31901`) all on 42.14 / picture off 29.05 / GE overlap off 44.62 / old sync 47.99 ms a step against gl2d's 42.14 / 29.04 / 44.63 / 47.98. **hwmem** (hw + `MGS2_GL_TEXKB=7168`: PPSSPP's heap left as the PSP's, 6,608 KB at the start): the same 745 lines, 52 picture lines and six shots, to the end, no 'no memory' line; its lowest sampled headroom 504 KB at vb 31,664. hwmem is tighter than the PSP: the larger pool lets the cache keep 2,286 textures, and its entry array (`ge/texcache.c` insert, doubled by realloc) takes about 256 KB more heap than with the PSP's 1,792 KB budget (arena 11,219 KB against 5,587 + 5,120 + about 512 in use and fragments). **hwmem_old** (gl2d's build, same cfg): first difference at vb 26,500 (ee 6c21438daa50e399, reference f1d87c9100b8d5f1; iop and vfb equal): PPSSPP ignores the write to address 0 that crashed the PSP, so the game goes on without 32 KB of the codec data. The per-vblank break samples (`main_psp.c` g_brk_max) cannot see a peak inside an IOP run: newlib gives the top back on free (gl2d's emulator run shows its highest break at vb 24,897, not 26,108), so the queue counts of sifq / sifq2 are the measure of the old peak.
- Delivery files: `prof/hw_gl2m/mgs2_gl2m.cfg` (gl2d's cfg under a 3-line gl2m header, b40b042d..), `prof/hw_gl2m/README_gl2m.txt`. EBOOT **308f4a56** (supersedes eecb1dda); overlays d00t 552e7e95, d01t 9a16b4ed, init 54ea7c6e, n_title a1c7a558, r_title 6b2626a3, r_tnk0 aa5fc9e3, w00a 71ac184c, w01a 8a46bc8d. `psp/main_psp.c` 9188493e (mgs2_log_crash); `psp/gl3.sh` b814932d (EXC, TTL, NOPROF=1 drops the profiled step, EXTRA adds cfg lines).
- **Catcher 3 (MGS2_EXC=3; PC preview and emulator [C], the PSP part untested):** the handler also stores control register 13 and the interrupt enable (mfic); it draws the record with interrupts off as catcher 2, then, for a user-mode thread with Status.IE on, mfic 1 and c13 0, sets Status.IE and spins at its kernel alias. uOFW read [C]: the general exception entry (exceptionman excep.S sub_0040) saves v0/v1/Cause/Status/EPC to c4/c5/c3/c2/c0 and jumps through ExcepManCB, no mtic; interruptman's intrExcepHandler saves the context at c14's area and breaks (0x3FF) only for kernel mode at a user EPC (andi KSU, sra EPC 31). The writer thread exc_writer (priority 0x10, 8 KB, a look every 0.2 s, sceIo only) writes crash.txt, finds the stopped thread by k0 inside a thread's stack block, puts the record (after the run's unwritten lines when it is the main thread) in log.txt, suspends it, draws two green lines (main thread only), writes battery.txt's "crash record saved" last, then scePowerTick every 10 s for 10 min. A crash in kernel mode, with interrupts off or in an interrupt handler keeps catcher 2's screen-only behaviour. PC preview `psp/exc3_test.sh`: 7 records, all fit (27 rows at most). The handler's call tree (callwalk) holds only the debug-screen code: no system call.
- **PPSSPP final (TAG f and fd, NOPROF=1; fd = hw + MGS2_EXC_DRY=33000) [C]:** in 434 / 434 + 127 / 127, digests 95 all 7516b81038cc0573; deck 370 / 370 + 63 / 63, 95 all d3a69e483e378792; hw and fd 434 / 434 + 127 / 127, 50 all 25efe5bcab7e30fa; every shot byte for byte gl3's (in 5, deck 4, hw 6, fd 6). crash.txt: "writer thread started (..., priority 0x10, a look every 200 ms)"; fd adds the dry run's record and "the dry run's record above reached this file 1269 s after power-on (vb 33000)", battery.txt a "dry run" line and log.txt the record: the record code and the writer work, and the game state is untouched.

The reading goes to task #72.
- **Ffm's own PPSSPP on the PC [C]:** MGS2T_gl3 was started there at about 20:00 (folder umd0:/, 3 minutes; its log.txt, battery.txt, crash.txt and a 32 MB split.500 are in Desktop\mg2 port\MGS2T_gl3). Its hash differs from vb 500: that PPSSPP's VFPU rounds toward zero (probes vadd / vsub / vmul RZ 4096 of 4096) where the PSP and the container's patched PPSSPP round to nearest (RN 4096). Expected; not a harness fault.
- **Self-test on the PSP (Ffm 2026-10-28 13:40 and 13:47: crash.txt, log.txt, battery.txt and a photo) [C]: catcher 3 FAILED; the PSP switched itself off about 10 s after the record.** The photo: the SELF-TEST record (the expected EPC; Status 60008613, user mode, interrupts on), row 2 "c13 00000000, c15 882ff800, c16 88231c84, ic 1", k1 00000000, the white "Interrupts are back on" text drawn, no green lines. crash.txt ends at "self-test started"; battery.txt has only its start lines (t 0; exc_writer 16/16 st 4 in the thread list); log.txt ends with the start lines. So the record was drawn and Status.IE set, and then nothing ran: the writer thread (a look every 0.2 s) never wrote. Cause [I]: the handler left the thread in kernel mode on the catcher's own stack in user memory, spinning, with no syscall context in the thread's slot (c16); its k1 was already 0. PSPLink (psplinkusb `psplink/exception_asm.S`, BSD) does three things first when the exception came from user mode: it moves to the thread's kernel stack (c15), links a 32-byte syscall context through c16, and sets k1 = 0; only then does it enable interrupts. The v26me3 catcher's self-test (ERET into a trap on its own stack, above) also ended with the PSP off.
- **The run uses MGS2_EXC=1 (catcher 2, as in gl3).** Ffm asked at 13:50 whether to run gl4 normally. The PC copy Desktop\mg2 port\MGS2T_gl4\mgs2.cfg was changed in place at 13:54 (MGS2_EXC=3 -> 1, plus one comment line); md5 35837766aeb7a9fb76b1e4c57edd68c0, was da1289e8. ## Build gl4b (2026-10-28; gl4 + crash catcher 3b, task #73; built 14:19; emulator-checked 14:36; delivered 15:02 after gl4 run 1's log was read, to Desktop\mg2 port\MGS2T_gl4b, all 15 md5s checked on the PC)

- **Why:** gl4's catcher 3 drew the self-test's record, turned interrupts on and then nothing ran; the PSP switched off about 10 s later (above). Catcher 3 had left the crashed thread in kernel mode, spinning on the catcher's own stack in user memory, with no system-call context in the thread's slot.
- **What 3b changes (only the end of the handler; `psp/exc3b.S` md5 53715810, `psp/exc3b.c` bcfcc816; the design and the credit are in both files' headers):** the record is drawn as before (interrupts off, the catcher's stack). Then, for a crash in user mode with interrupts on (mgs2_exc_screen's test, as catcher 3), the thread is handed back to the system the way PSPLink does it (psplinkusb `psplink/exception_asm.S` and `exception.c` psplinkTrap, BSD licence, Copyright (c) 2005 James F): it moves to its own kernel stack (control register 15), writes there the 32-byte system-call context the kernel's system-call entry writes (Status, EPC, sp, ra, k1, FCSR 0, v0 0, the link; uOFW interruptman start.S syscallExcepHandler) and links it into the thread's slot (control register 16), sets k1 = 0, turns Status.IE on and calls the kernel's own sceKernelSleepThread (ThreadManForUser NID 0x9ACE131E, found at the start with sctrlHENFindFunction; a kernel address or none) in a loop that counts any return (mgs2_exc_sleep[1], the last result in [2]). The thread is then, to the system, a thread asleep inside a system call; the writer thread (priority 0x10) saves the record. No syscall instruction (the kernel breaks on one from kernel mode), no new module, no register write, no new kernel call. Not found at the start: 3b behaves as catcher 2 (the screen only) and says so.
- **Screen:** the writer's first green line, "The system runs again: the writer thread is saving the record.", comes before any system call when the stopped thread is the main thread, so a photo shows whether the threads ran even if a later step fails; then "Saved to crash.txt and log.txt N s after power-on." and "The stopped thread sleeps and is suspended; HOME quits." crash.txt's line adds the stopped thread's status and wait type (a sleeping thread: wait type 1, sleep) and the sleep's return count.
- **PC preview [C]:** `C=exc3b sh psp/exc3_test.sh /home/claude/exc3b_test` (psp/exc3b_test.c a4c822e8, psp/exc3_test.sh d8ee81ee now takes C=): every record fits (cases: the self-test, a real crash, sleep not found, a sleep that returned twice); the self-test screen is 28 rows (record 22, white text 3, green 3).
- **Build [C]:** `GL3=/home/claude/gl4b EXC=exc3b TTL="MGS2 harness gl4b crash catcher 3b" sh psp/gl3.sh d` (gl4's sources otherwise: ge/xlate.c 9503b406, ge/vu1conv.c 18465873, runtime/iop_host.h a105b64e, runtime/sif.h aa69f363, psp/main_psp.c 9188493e). EBOOT **ee2b4cc9**; overlays as gl4's except ovl_w00a c1e23f86 (gl4 71ac184c): one word differs, the load of g_opt, which moved 0x590 with the larger catcher. Linked handler (callwalk from mgs2_exc_handler): the same functions as gl4's catcher 3 (the pspDebugScreen calls, x_report, code_at, decoded, xl_psp_gl_screen) and one indirect call, the jalr to the sleep; the code is exc3b.S's.
- **Emulator [C]** (`/home/claude/gl4b/emu`, 14:23-14:36, container only; gl4's four runs with the same settings: in_f, deck_f, hw_f, hw_fd as inf, deckf, hwf, hwfd): every run to its end; the picture digest lines (95 / 95 / 50 / 50), the hash and vuh lines (434 / 370 / 434 / 434 hash lines) and the shots (5 / 4 / 6 / 6) are byte for byte gl4's runs' (the md5 of each set equal), so the catcher's change moves nothing in the game or the picture (gl4's runs: exact against the x86 references, "Build gl4"). PPSSPP does not run the kernel call: "crash catcher: off (emulator: PPSSPP does not run the kernel call)", the writer thread starts; hw_fd's dry run (a made-up record, no exception) printed its record and the run went on.

## Build gl5 (2026-10-28; gl4c + the P07 converter (the codec faces), renderer_notes §11.18, and §11.17's subtitle fix; task #82; Ffm chose "Deliver now" on the card of 23:00 (tapped 23:05), then "pause wen possible, we continue friday"; emulator-checked along the whole route 23:02-23:22; delivered 23:23 to Desktop\mg2 port\MGS2T_gl5, all 15 md5s checked on the PC)

- **Build [C]:** `GL3=/home/claude/gl5 H2D=/home/claude/h2c/d WPD=/home/claude/v31/WPL EXC=exc3b TTL="MGS2 harness gl5 codec faces" sh psp/gl3.sh d` (container /home/claude/gl5/d): EBOOT **f0141000** (3,985,552 bytes). ## PPSSPP master: the PSP's pictures in the container (2026-10-29 / 30; task #88, Ffm chose "Start now" on the card of 2026-10-29 02:35)
- **Build (container, ephemeral):** master 83e616ea, a shallow clone with submodules (the test-data ones pspautotests and frametests removed), `tools/ppsspp_master_vfpu_rn.diff` (VFPU rounding, `PPSSPP_VFPU_RN=1`) and `tools/ppsspp_master_fpu_fs.diff` (the FPU under FCR31 FS as the PSP-3000, `PPSSPP_FPU_FS=1`), then the first diff's cmake line and `ninja PPSSPPHeadless` (needs libgl1-mesa-dev). `tools/dev/ppsspp_master_run.sh <game dir> <out>` runs a delivery's files (mgs2.cfg with MGS2_EXC=0) with both switches and the software GE, and stops PPSSPP once the log's end lines are written (the harness then waits on its end screen, as on the PSP). gl4b's route 576 s, gl4c's 677 s.
- **Result [C]:** gl4c and gl4b give the PSP's own shots byte for byte (12 / 12) and every line the logs share for checking: hash (vb, ee, iop, vfb), vuh, picture (frame digests and totals), split, "probe fpu zero" (details: research2_port_plan.md "Results"). Without the FPU patch vfb was 50 higher from vb 15,794 on (master runs FS as FTZ + DAZ; the PSP flushes results only). Still different: VFPU special values in the probes (the PSP's NaN 0x7f800001, the vcmp read delay), not seen in the game's state or pictures. The patched 1.20.4 (`ppsspp_src`) stays for PspProf timing.

## Build gl6 (prep 2026-10-30; gl5 + the codec speed-ups A-D of codec_speed.md "Results 2026-10-30", taking turns; task #93; waits for gl5's log and Ffm's OK)

- **Why:** the PSP's own answer for each speed-up. The model says a codec step goes from 132.5 to 115.6 ms (A area memo -8.6, B flags-only test -2.0, C palette memo -2.2, D V4-8 decode -4.4, each added to the one before), but its I-miss weight (125 cycles) is not the PSP's (~73, probe 12), and these changes remove instructions more than misses. A, B and C act on every texture lookup (rooms, guards, the deck), so the settings take turns from vb 1,000, not only in the calls: one run also times them on the opening, the story calls and the aft deck (window lines; the scenes change there, so each window is compared with its neighbours).
- **Build [C]:** `GL3=/home/claude/gl6 H2D=/home/claude/h2c/d WPD=/home/claude/v31/WPL EXC=exc3b TTL="MGS2 harness gl6 codec speed-ups" sh psp/gl3.sh d` (container /home/claude/gl6/d): EBOOT **434fbd60** (3,991,572 bytes; gl5 3,985,552). The game's objects (gen*.o, boot_rt.o, iop*.o) equal gl5's; changed: ge_gsup2, ge_texcache, ge_xlate, main_psp (the timing settings 17-21 by name), xl_psp (the switches). The eight overlays all differ from gl5's (linked for the new EBOOT's end; ovl_w00a 4,384,688 bytes as gl5's).
- **cfg (`prof/hw_gl6/mgs2_gl6.cfg` 36d676bf, delivered as mgs2.cfg):** gl5's (route, pad, pack, vblanks 40000, deck 34000, hashes, the six shots, MGS2_GL_DIG=30990:31399, catcher 3b, texture pool 2,688 KB) plus `MGS2_OPT_CYCLE=1000:100:ffffffff,fffeffff,fffdffff,fffbffff,fff7ffff,fff0ffff`: all four on (cfg 0), A off (17), B off (18), C off (19), D off (20), all four off as gl5 (21), 100 vblanks each in turn. On PPSSPP master with our two patches (the PSP's own pictures, `research2_port_plan.md` "Results") gl5 and gl6 give that shot byte for byte, and gl6 three runs in a row the same (s1/mst_m5a, m6a-c); master's shot differs from 1.20.4's in about 4,400 pixels. So shots are compared on master from now on. **Master, whole route [C]:** gl5's and gl6's delivery files (cfg b537da44 and 36d676bf, MGS2_EXC=0; `tools/dev/ppsspp_master_run.sh`, container s1/mst_g5full and mst_g6full, 816 and 796 s): the six shots byte for byte the same, 438 / 438 hash and 311 / 311 vuh lines, the 205 digests (e5535d8d57747651) and every other picture line equal, no split, both to the end. The PSP's GS walk gained 1.06 (deck), 0.71 (inside), 0.81 / 0.86 (calls) ms a step where the model's translator gained 3.37 / 2.41 / 1.66: about a third on the deck and inside, half in the calls [C]. Unchanged code moved by placement (deck: picture +0.66, logic +0.21) [C]. The model's gain was nearly all instructions (-3.0 ms; I-misses -0.5, D-misses +0.8); the fit prices every instruction at 1.65 cycles, an average that includes stalls, and the removed bookkeeping (record writes, table walks) is simple integer and store work nearer 1 cycle [I]: at 1 cycle the deck's 3.4 would be about 2 [I]. The off turns again cost more than the model's no-PGO figures (copy off +3.7 against +2.4 on the deck): paths the all-on PGO profile made cold, as with gl11's lane 3 [I]. So for small cuts of simple instructions the model can overstate the PSP gain two to three times; the earlier cuts (lanes, step c, trims) matched within about 15% on the deck and in the calls. No fault: every check exact. Rule from here: size Stage C's steps with the model, then judge each by the PSP's all-on windows against the build before.

- **Build [C]:** `psp/gl14mp.sh /home/claude/sbg14mp` on gl14's build d (/home/claude/sbg14/d): gl14's objects as they are; main_psp.o compiled again with a title of the same length; gl1_glue.o with five functions renamed (me_probe.c gives them); me_probe.o, the ME's unit (me_probe_me.c + me_probe_kern.c + me_rest.S, `ld -r`, checked as v30me6.sh checks its own) and v30me6c's bring-up (psp/me/hle_me_mgs2.c -DME_REST -DME_SONY -DME_SONY_BOOT -DME_KADDR) after gl14's objects; `--wrap=hw_probe`, `-lpspdmac`. Pads 0x14f0, 0x1d4, 0x1d98, 0x1cf4 (tools/dev/padfit.py): gl14's 3,219 code sections of its own objects at gl14's addresses, the library code +0x9f88 (196 of 294 sections), data moved by multiples of 8 KB (g_mem 0x09084100, gl14 0x09076100), ovl_arena 0x08c00000 (gl14 0x08bf2000), each overlay gl14's size; no new library function (sce* calls apart). - **Build 1's memory problem [C]:** the first build took the boot probe's buffers from the heap and freed them; its ME block stayed in the heap above them (malloc arena 2,140 KB at the start, 2,055 KB free in 3 chunks), so the game's pools went higher: the lowest headroom 5,192 KB at vb 1 in 1.20.4 against gl14n's 5,612 KB (420 KB less), which the PSP's 376 KB [I] could not give. Build 2 takes the buffers from game RAM before the game is loaded (8 MB at g_mem + 16 MB, checked zero before and zeroed again after) and the 80 KB block (control block, ME region, ring) from user memory outside the heap.

- **The ME did not start [C]:** "heartbeat DEAD (boots 0->1, state 1, heartbeat 0->0, reset reg was 00000040)", stage 3 of 3, check and control-block words right, no exception recorded, power callback 1. So the ME ran xme_me_main up to `boots++; state = 1` and never reached its first heartbeat. The arena is in the eDRAM, so the main CPU made the whole picture itself (each transfer walked from RAM as it ended: 223,296 records), exactly; the ME's half of the turns never ran.
- **Cause [C in code]:** the ME's CP0 Count reads a constant until the ME writes it (me_probe_notes.md 3; v26me2's "Count 0.0 MHz"). Probes 1 and 2 write `mtc0 $zero, $9` when their loop starts; psp/xme_me.c did not, so the first wait's `me_spin` (5 us on Count) never ended. **Fix (2026-11-18, in main/ for gl19):** xme_me_main starts Count before its first write; `me_spin` also stops after `ticks` rounds (each round is 7 instructions, so a running Count always ends it first, and a stopped one can no longer hold the ME); tools/xme_me_chk.py allows exactly that one `mtc0 zero,$9` (PASS, 133 instructions); xl_psp.c prints the loop's shared words (heart, place, idle, tail, head) after a failed start.
- **Speed, the main CPU alone with the split's bookkeeping and no PGO in the rebuilt tree [C], ms a step against gl17 run 2:** deck waiting (vb 29,900) 168.55 / 147.78, the rain (30,900) 162.52 / 143.11, inside (33,500) 105.33 / 89.39, vb 35,000 59.79 / 50.64: +14-19%. What the ME saves: still UNKNOWN (gl19 with the fix).

- **Reading [I]:** the main CPU now waits for the ME. By the code, its first copy plan of a step waits until the ME has walked the step before (psp/xl_psp.c mq_chk_end: one arena, reset after each walk), and the ME starts a frame only after the GE has drawn every list before, its own last one included (ge/xme.c xme_hook: one frame memory). So the two overlap only from the hook to the step's first transfer. In the opening at vb 12,300 the main CPU's own walk was about 20 ms a step (logic 59.8 -> 39.3) and it waited 36 ms: the ME's time a step was 36 ms plus that overlap (0 to 61 ms), 1.8x to 4.8x the main CPU's walk, its copies and GE waits included. The ME's split and the overlap's length are UNKNOWN: gl20 adds ME-side timers (its Count) and the main CPU's time from the hook to its first wait.

- **The cuts on the PSP [C, each cut turn against the mean of the same maker's off turns 200 vb before and after, scenes with neighbours within 8% only]:** deck waiting and rain, main CPU turns: b -5.8 ms a step (-3.2%; 2 pairs, -6.9 and -4.6), f -5.6 (1 pair); ME turns: b -2.1 (3 pairs), f +5.8 (2 pairs, noisy). Then gl21: cut b in every turn from the deck (DEVIATIONS G1) and, if the timers show the ME has time to spare, two arena halves so that the two sides overlap.

Emulator [C]: 434 / 434 hashes and 311 vuh equal (the game is untouched), but only 1 of 150 frame digests equals the twin's (lists equal, frame data differ), and of 16 shots against the exact run's: 4 equal (to vb 23,263), 2 with 1-step differences of 1 (24,783, 31,112), 10 with 200-680 pixels off by 2+ in a few small boxes (from vb 30,500 on always one 40 x 8 box in the bottom-right corner; at 24,244 a 150 x 85 box; at 29,500 and 36,941 a top band too) [C]. Plans 119,102, wraps 3,029 / 1,631, waits for the oldest walk 2,299, at most 2 walks in flight, live at most 3,071 KB / 4,095 extents. Cause [I]: the walks read part of their data straight from RAM, not from the copy: 214,866 image runs and 959,556 reads of the GS map (24.1 GB of image data left in RAM over the run, 124,231 pieces); with the ring those reads come up to a step late, after the next step's game code changed some of them. A debug pass (gl21d, MGS2_ME_PIC_RCHK, exact) hashed the map's pieces at walk time and at the ring's latest time: 16 and 18 changed (the boot's 0x0c00000 refill, plus 11a09f0-11a53c0 at vb 20,694 and 26,068), so the late data is mostly outside what rchk hashed (its marks left out 0x12c0000-0x1430000 and 0x1ed0000-0x1ee0000, and the runs read from RAM). A deterministic ring needs every RAM read of a walk to come from its copy (about 1.3 MB of image data a step more to copy [C, the run's total / 18,900 steps]) or a renderer that does not walk the PS2's packets (Phase 2). Not worth building before gl21's ME timers say how much the overlap could give.

The ME helps on the deck (-8%), in the rain (-14%) and the calls (-13%) and loses in the black opening, the opening and calls 2 (+16..+25%).
- **The ME's timers [C, per step, ME turns; the printed idle includes the whole main turn before, because the ME's one idle wait spans it: idle here = turn minus the rest]:** deck: turn 66.1, ME walks 34.5, records 3.4, hooks 19.3 of which GE waits 16.1, idle about 9; the main CPU waited for the arena 19.9 and the fills 2.7; from a hook to the main CPU's first copy plan 0.66 ms. Whole run's ME turns 1,067 s: walks 393 s, hooks 169 s (GE waits 148 s), records 66 s.
- **Reading [I, from the code]:** the GE wait is in xme_hook's begin (ge/xme.c: a frame begins only when every list before has been drawn, one frame memory). The ME learns the GE is done only from ge_done, which the main CPU sets in mq_service (psp/xl_psp.c) when its sceGeDrawSync(1) poll sees the list finished, and the ME's list reaches the GE only when mq_service submits it. The main CPU calls mq_service while it waits (every 50 us), at its hooks and at each transfer end, but not while it runs game logic: so the 16 / 41 ms hold both the GE's real drawing and the main CPU's notice and submit delays. Their split is UNKNOWN.
- **Next:** gl22 = gl21 + timestamps of each list's submit, the GE's finish (GE finish callback) and the main CPU's notice + the GE finish callback setting ge_done itself (MGS2_ME_PIC_GEFAST), off and on in alternate ME turns; the ME-turn line prints idle as turn minus the rest.

## Build gl22 (2026-11-19; gl21 + the GE's finish callback telling the ME at once, in alternate ME turns, + GE drawing timestamps; tree mgs2/main/ (build_gl22))
- **Why:** gl21's ME waited 16.1 ms a deck step (41.5 rain) in its frame begin for the GE; the split between the GE's real drawing and the main CPU's notice + submit delays was UNKNOWN (gl21 "Reading").
- **Change (psp/xl_psp.c 0b2897ba = gl21's 46ddc4b9 + scratchpad gefast/apply.py, copy in main/attic/gefast_gl22/apply.py 425487f2):** sceGuSetCallback(GU_CALLBACK_FINISH, ge_fin_cb) in gl_setup; ge_fin_cb stores the time and counts, and with ge_fast on (ME turns only, !mq_self, a list out: gl_ge) sets ge_done = mq_subm; mq_submit stamps the submit; ge_noted() (before gl_show in mq_service and mq_ge_finish) adds per list submit->finish (the GE's drawing) and finish->notice, by bucket (0 ME turn fast off, 1 ME turn fast on, 2 main CPU turn). One list is out at a time (mq_service submits only when !gl_ge), so the finish of the one list out means every list before is done; a finish racing mq_submit's counters only leaves ge_done low (the main CPU then notices as before). MGS2_ME_PIC_GEFAST: 0 off, 1 always, 2 in turns (mq_turn_hook "to the ME": ge_fast = (vb / (2 x turn)) & 1: on in the ME turns that start at vb 400j + 200, off in those at 400j). me turn line suffix "gl22: idle in this turn (turn minus the rest); GE fast f: lists n, drawing d ms, seen s ms after its finish" (sums over the turn); mq_report "me picture GE (gl22)" lines per bucket + callback count.

## Build gl23 (2026-11-19; gl22 + two frames on the GE side in turns + GEFAST always; tree mgs2/main/ (build_gl23))
- **Why:** gl22's reading: the ME's frame begin waited until the GE had drawn the last list (one frame on the GE side), so the GE's drawing (22-27 ms a deck frame) and the main CPU's late submit and notice sat on the ME's path, and the main CPU waited about 20 ms a step for the ME's walk.
- **Change (scratchpad ge2/apply.py, copy main/attic/ge2_gl23/apply.py 3165e25c):** ge/xme.h: XmeSh.ge2 (the main CPU's switch, uncached), XmeCtx fm[2] / fl[2] / fm_arena / fm_nb / fm_nv (host's) and fm_k / tk_fb / n_ge2 (the ME's). ge/xme.c xme_hook begin: with ge2 and fm[1], wait only until every list but the last is released (listed - ge_done <= 1), then guard the textures of the frame just ended (tk_guard(tk_fb)), switch to the other frame memory (gb_arena on glf.o) and list (p->gxl = fl[k]); otherwise tk_guard(TK_NO_GUARD) as before. ge/texcache.c: TkCache.guard, tk_now, tk_guard; an entry is evicted only if frame < now - 1 and frame < guard. psp/xl_psp.c: gl_vol2 (2,048 KB, memalign from the heap: the volatile block is full) + gxl2 when MGS2_ME_PIC_GE2 > 0; XS(mq)->ge2 set at an ME turn's start (2: on in the ME turns that start at vb 400j + 200), 0 at the hand-over to the main CPU; GE buckets by GE2; the me turn suffix "GE2 g: lists, drawing, seen ...; begun while the GE drew the frame before n"; report line "me picture GE (gl23)". gx_frame resets the list's GE state at each frame (gelist.c), so two GxLists are safe; one list is out at a time, so GEFAST's ge_done = mq_subm stays right.
- **Checks [C]:** tools/mesec_chk.py now takes gxl2 as an optional ME line object (LN_OPT; gl22 and gl23 both PASS) and psp.sh stops on a mesec_chk FAIL (gl22's psp.sh only printed it); xme_me_chk PASS, me_call_chk PASS. - **Gain small [C]:** windows, ME turns vs gl22: deck -0.6%, rain -0.8%, calls -2.5% (GE2 in half the ME turns). Steady deck stretch vb 26,200-28,600, per step (per-turn wall / 100): GE2 on 49.8 ms (7 turns) vs off 51.1 (6 turns), -2.5%; the ME's GE wait 9.6 -> 0.0 ms but its idle 0.4-3.9 -> 9-12.6; the main CPU's wait for the arena 23.5 in both; ME walk 33.4 in both. Rain (29,200-31,200): on 115-127 ms vs off 114-128 per step, GE wait 17-18 -> 0, idle +12-17, arena wait about the same.
- **Reading [C from the turn lines + I from the code]:** the lock-step is the copy arena, not the GE: the main CPU's first copy plan of a step comes 0.7 ms after its hook and must wait until the ME has walked the step before (one arena), so the walk (33 ms a deck step) runs while the main CPU waits (23.5) instead of while it runs the next step's logic (its own work is about 26 ms a step: wall 49.8 minus waits). Per step: main 26 + wait 23.5. The ME idles about 10 ms a step while the main CPU runs logic (nothing to walk yet). Overlapping the walk with the next step needs two arenas, and an exact walk a step late: gl21r's ring was exact in the game but not in the picture (HUD-size boxes) because the walk also reads game RAM directly, which the next step changes. Next: find which RAM a late walk reads that changed (x86 twin / emulator), copy just that at the hook; with an exact two-arena walk the deck step would be about max(main 26-30, ME 38-40) [I, about -20%].
- **Counters:** 30% of the ME-turn lines carry stale ME counts (hooks 0, lists -3, GE waits -50, a GE-wait time of 5.6e13): the main CPU reads the ME's counts before they reach RAM; times are mostly right. Fix in the next build: read the counts after the hand-over's write-back (or from the uncached alias).

## Build gl24 (2026-11-19; gl23 + two copy areas in turns + review fixes; tree mgs2/main/ (build_gl24b); delivered as MGS2T_gl24)
- **Why:** gl23's run showed the lock-step is the single copy arena. The main CPU's first copy plan of a step waits until the ME has walked the step before, so it waits about 23.5 ms a deck step.
- **Check first (gl24d, debug build; attic lchk_gl24d/README.md):** every byte range a hook walk reads from RAM outside its copies was hashed again at the next hook. Over 7,217 walks on the emulator to vb 31,400, the only changes were at vb 1 (the boot's refill) and vb 3815 (a map range inside xc_keep 1, which real builds copy anyway). Nothing changed on the deck [C, emulator; build_gl24d/emu/log.txt 36b790aa]. A change made and undone inside one step cannot be seen by this check [I].
- **Change (attic two_gl24/gl24_apply.py 47006a57, then gl24b_apply.py 1eff68ce):**
  - `MGS2_ME_PIC_TWO` (2 = every other ME turn): the arena (3,072 KB) and its 4,096 extents split into two halves.
  - A step plans into one half while the walk posted at the last hook holds the other. The main CPU waits for the walk before at the next hook's start (wait kind MW_HOOK).
  - A full half spills into the other once its walk is done. After gl23's synchronous fallback the step gets the whole arena.
  - Records keep absolute extent indices, so the ME side is unchanged.
  - The ME's idle time is moved to uncached XmeSh words t_idle_q/lo/hi (a seqlock).
  - GE2=1 (two frames on the GE side in every ME turn); GEFAST=1.
- **Review (two independent adversarial passes before shipping).** Fixed in gl24b:
  1. mq_idle's spin could hang the main CPU for good: the emulator's stand-in loop thread runs at priority 0x30, below the main thread, so it could never finish its write. Now a bounded yield.
  2. gl23's stale ME turn counts were really caused by `n_sync++` and `t_k[XE_SYNC]` written AFTER the hand-over's whole-cache write-back, not by the idle write. The write-back now runs in xme_run after them. This is unproven until the PSP log; PPSSPP has no cache, so the emulator cannot show it.
  3. A lone record larger than a half now gets the whole arena instead of a walk from RAM.
  4. A warm take-back now returns to one area.
  5. Walk-peak statistics are now per walk, and the report gives the whole arena's sizes.
  Left as is: the turn line's "hand-over" time no longer includes the write-back. It now shows up in "idle in this turn", so it is not comparable with gl23.

## Build gl27 (2026-11-19; gl26 with the screen-flip wait (the black marks) + the walk split timers off; tree mgs2/main/ (build_gl27); delivered as MGS2T_gl27)
- **Cause found in the code [C: code; the PSP effect I]:** gl_show hands a finished buffer to the display with PSP_DISPLAY_SETBUF_NEXTFRAME, so the screen scans the OLD buffer until the next vblank; mq_service gave the GE the next list in the same call when the ME's next result was already listed (TWO + GE2 make that common in ME turns), and that list clears to black and draws into the buffer still on the screen. Ffm's marks (19:52: black, shadow-like, flickering, coming and going every few seconds) fit: ME turns last ~4.5 s, main CPU turns ~7.5 s on the deck. Shots can't show it: xl_psp_gl_vblank drains the loop and waits for the GE before saving (the earlier back-to-back shots plan was dropped).
- **Changes:** (1) gl_show records sceDisplayGetVcount(); mq_service holds the next list while the vcount is unchanged (MGS2_GL_FLIPWAIT 0 off and counted / 1 always / 2 every other ME turn and the main turn after it, as GE2=2's parity; MGS2_GL_FLIPWAIT_FROM vb: always on from there). The shot path and mq_end use flip_force (no wait, as gl26). Per-turn lines 'me turn vb A-B gl27 display' / 'main turn vb A-B gl27 display' (flip on/off, lists held and ms held, lists drawn into the shown buffer); end line 'picture display (gl27)'. gl_list (no loop) only counts. (2) XmeCtx gains int ws_on at its end (sizeof still 0x8400); xme_walk passes no clock to xm_tsplit unless MGS2_ME_SPLIT=1, so gl26's split timers cost nothing. cfg/mgs2_gl27.cfg (a97f7cd5) = gl26's + FLIPWAIT=2, FROM=30500 (A/B before Snake's walk for the cost and the counted cases; all on during the walk for Ffm's eyes).

Left as nits: a hold open across a turn boundary puts its remainder (under a vblank) in the bucket total but in no turn line; a cold take-back drops an open hold's time.

- **Harness rules adopted from this build (lead, 2026-11-23):**
  1. "A lever is in a build only when its code is shown in the PSP ELF (a symbol, the disassembly, or a counter in the log), not
  when its twin arm passes: boot_rt.o compiles rt/ ahead of runtime/, so a change in runtime/kernel.h, ee_io.h, iop_host.h or
  sif.h reaches the PC twin only."
  2. "After every relink compare `readelf -l` with the last delivered build's: the same number of LOAD segments, the first R E with
  FileSiz = MemSiz. A pad in a linker script is a section with contents, never NOBITS in front of loaded sections."
  3. "After any change of statics, compare the hot tables' addresses mod 0x2000 with the last build's from the link map before any
  model run; judge a retrain against the last build's own windows at the same turns and kinds (one extra cfg for the old
  build), first."
  4. "Profile runs use PPSSPPHeadless_fcr; a profiled run whose hash lines differ is a finding, not noise."
